# Judgments

The decisions this implementation makes where Agent Plugins Extended leaves the choice to an
implementation (AGENTS.md, E4; docs/SPEC.md, "Judgments and deviations"): how the kit shapes an
API, orders work or words an issue the spec does not word. Each row names the code that holds it.
Its "Why" is `Spec J<chapter>.<n>` when a spec judgment decides the point (the row then records
only what is the kit's own on top of it), or says it is the implementation's choice. A behaviour
the spec should state is taken to the spec, not decided here (E1a). A judgment is never counted as
compatibility.

| Where                         | Judgment                                                                                          | Why                                                                                       |
| ----------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `src/host/sdk-hooks.ts`, `src/host/sdk-output.ts`, `tests/host/sdk-hooks.test.ts`, `tests/host/sdk-output.test.ts` | `sdkHooks` registers one matcherless callback per event that dispatches to the session; the matcher's `timeout` is the longest handler limit of the event at build; an input the event's schema refuses throws. `toSdkHookOutput` joins several hooks' `additionalContext` and `systemMessage` with a newline and terminal sequences with nothing, writes an exit-2 block as `deny` (on `PreModelSwitch` a `defer` is dropped, which it does not take), and adds no `decision` for a block by `continue: false`. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: how a host that merged several plugins' hooks answers the Agent SDK as one callback (one matcherless callback per event, longest handler timeout, joined outputs); the SDK pages carry no requirement ids. |
| `src/settings/context.ts`, `tests/settings/policy-context.test.ts` | `policyContext(facts)` fills each `PolicyContext` fact the host leaves out with the value that grants the least: a `console` (API-key) account without Pro, no managed settings and no parent merge, no folder trusted, an empty environment, no default channel allowlist, and `claude-plugins-official` as the one official marketplace. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (J13.29 decides only the untrusted-folder default): every other `PolicyContext` fact the host leaves out takes the least-granting value, so no rule, channel or auto-update turns on unasked. |
| `src/mods/load/guard-protection.ts` | The host classifies managed tool descriptors and additional protected rows through trusted identity callbacks on the original event; a failed lookup skips the user hook. A binding token prevents an older unload from disabling a newer guard. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: the host classifies managed tool descriptors and further protected rows through trusted identity callbacks on the original event, a failed lookup skips the user hook, and a binding token keeps an older unload from disabling a newer guard (§14.9.2 item 3 names what is protected, not identity fields or overlapping reloads). |
| `src/mods/chain/dispatch.ts` | `next.origin` is a frozen copy rather than the embedding caller's mutable object. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: `next.origin` is a frozen copy, not the embedding caller's mutable object (§10.6.1 item 3 is silent on who owns the object). |
| `src/mods/chain/dispatch.ts`, `src/foundation/freeze.ts` (`frozenCopy`) | Hooks get a frozen deep copy of the host's input; the host's own object is not frozen. Cycles and shared objects are kept, and the copy does not recurse | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: hooks get a frozen deep copy of the host's input and the host's own object is not frozen; cycles and shared objects are kept (§10.6 says `e` is deeply frozen, not whose object). |
| `src/mods/chain/invoke.ts` | A hook that returns its downstream result unchanged is not judged again: an observer that passes on an inner mod's drop or answer is not reported, and a result is checked once (`ui.render` is not revalidated per hook) | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: a hook that returns its downstream result unchanged is not judged again, so a pass-through is not reported and a result is checked once. |
| `src/mods/chain/registry.ts`, `src/mods/load/mod-set.ts`, `src/mods/load/order.ts` | A mod keeps its slot in its tier when it is registered again (a reload, a fresh copy, `loadMod`); the loader passes the policy's `position`, and the built-in guard is first through position `-Infinity` | Spec J10.9. The loader passes the policy's `position`, and the built-in guard is first through position `-Infinity`. |
| `src/mods/chain/matcher.ts`, `src/mods/chain/match-local.ts` | A RegExp from another realm is recognised by brand; an object with a `$regexp` key is a serialised expression, and one whose `$regexp` or `flags` is not a string is an issue, not a match; any other object is a partial of an object field, its fields tested as a matcher's (a regular expression inside it runs in the `MatcherEngine` too); against an array field a value holds when some element matches | Spec §10.13.3 item 1 gives the four kinds (value, array, regular expression, partial object) and the array-field rule; recognising a foreign RegExp by brand and refusing a broken `$regexp` are the kit's. |
| `src/mods/chain/validate.ts`, `src/mods/load/scan/rules.ts` | The static scan applies the chain's own telemetry-filter rule (`lacksTelemetryFilter`), so a filter whose `to` accepts the collector passes both | Spec J10.19. The scan reuses the chain's `lacksTelemetryFilter`, so the scan never refuses what the chain accepts. |
| `src/mods/ui/elements/children.ts` | A Box (any element that holds children) refuses a child that is not a string, an element or Claude Code's drawing, and refuses an unknown element type | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: a Box refuses a child that is not a string, an element or Claude Code's drawing, and an unknown element type (§10.24 item 1 says a child where none goes fails, not which values a Box child may be). |
| `src/mods/ui/elements/raster.ts`, `tests/mods/ui/elements-media.test.ts` | A `Raster`'s `cells` must be base64 that decodes to exactly `columns × rows × 12` bytes (three little-endian 32-bit numbers a cell), else the tree does not validate: `Raster prop "cells" must be base64 of exactly columns × rows × 3 unsigned 32-bit numbers (<n> bytes)`; a `Raster` without `cells` is not checked | Spec J10.105. The refusal wording is the kit's, and a `Raster` without `cells` is not checked. |
| `src/mods/ui/toast.ts`, `tests/mods/ui/toast.test.ts` | Past 50 toasts waiting behind a hold, the oldest leaves, with the debug line `$.ui.toast (<mod>): <text> left the stack: more than 50 toasts wait behind a hold` | Spec J10.107. The debug line `$.ui.toast (<mod>): <text> left the stack: more than 50 toasts wait behind a hold` is the kit's wording. |
| `src/mods/api/theme.ts`, `src/mods/ui/sites/backdrop.ts`, `tests/mods/api/theme.test.ts` | declarations 2.1.321: `glass` is `{ blur 0–64 (16), tint (a raw color), opacity 0–1 (0.6) }` or `false`, anything else rejects `$.theme.set`; the session's backdrop and the `Backdrop` site's `e.props` always carry `glass` with its defaults filled in, or `false` | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: declarations 2.1.321 `ThemeBackdropGlass`: `glass` is `{ blur 0–64 (16), tint, opacity 0–1 (0.6) }` or `false`, anything else rejects `$.theme.set` (§10.25.6 says `e.props` carry `glass` with its defaults filled in, not the ranges or defaults). |
| `src/mods/load/scan/`, `src/mods/load/commands/`, `src/format/mod-files.ts`, `tests/mods/load/validate-325.test.ts` | oracle 2.1.325: the messages of `$ itself is bound to a name (bound, passed, spread, returned or read)` (a bare `$` bound or destructured), `a computed or optional member access on $`, a second `modules` entry, a bare import, an imported file not named like code, a dynamic import, `$.<ns> is used as a value (...)`, `on("<event>") is registered twice without a matcher; the first is at <file>:<line>`, a streaming hook that is not an async generator, the telemetry stream rule and `<plugin>.<key> is not declared: the manifest's types contract must name it ...`; the lines `answers its own command:` (a `command.run` hook whose matcher names a command), `hooks telemetry for:` (each telemetry hook's `to`, `anthropic` without one), `state of other plugins, not checked (run validate in a session with them enabled):`, `types <path> declares on $:` (`nothing (no EngineInterface member)` when none) and `declares state:` (the manifest's `notes`); a telemetry event and a glob or negation selecting a gating event is gating; a `$.state.get` or `set` ref written as a `{ plugin, key }` literal is listed and checked as `plugin.key`, another plugin's value not checked; a `--json` error or warning is `{ path, message }` and a gating hook `{ module, pattern, hook, hasCatch }` with `pattern` as `on` was given and `hook` with its matcher. A refusal of a rule of §10.4.2's table (`no-register`, `event-not-literal`, `unknown-event`, `on-shadowed`, `on-twice`, `not-generator`, `missing-filter`, `state-undeclared`, `dollar-as-value`, `dollar-escapes`) is printed at `modules.<entry>` as `<plugin>: <absolute file>:<line>: <message>; $ is always spelled $.noun.event(...) at the call site, on is always on("<event>", hook), and next.to always next.to(e, "<tier>")` (oracle 2.1.326), `<plugin>` the last segment of `ValidateInput.root`, the line where the offending node starts, line 1 for a rule of the whole file (a missing `register`, an undeclared value read through `$.state`); without `root` the lead names `target`; a parse failure, an import, `require`, a `Client` or an unreadable hook keeps its `<file>: <message>` line at the file's path | Spec J10.2, J17.15. The rule ids (`no-register`, `event-not-literal`, ...) and keeping a parse failure, an import, `require`, a `Client` or an unreadable hook at the file's path are the kit's. |
| `src/mods/api/http-redirects.ts`, `src/mods/api/http.ts` | A redirect to a URL that is not http(s) is refused (`$.http.fetch: a redirect to <url> is not an http or https URL`); a method a hook passes on is upper-cased | Spec J10.143. A method a hook passes on is upper-cased. |
| `src/mods/api/fetch-policy.ts`, `src/mods/api/http.ts` | Network policy (`fetchPolicy`) is asked per request and per redirect hop; without host facts nothing is stopped | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: the network policy (`fetchPolicy`) is asked per request and per redirect hop, and without host facts nothing is stopped (§14.9.3 says the policy covers `$.http.fetch`, not per hop). |
| `src/mods/load/wiring.ts` | Native (host) hooks the chain refuses make `registerNative` throw `<name>: <issues>` | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: native host hooks the chain refuses make `registerNative` throw `<name>: <issues>`. |
| `src/mods/load/loader.ts` | When the host's step after registration (`onLoaded`, which fires `ui.resolve`) throws, the mod is unloaded and the line is `<mod>: hooks module did not load: <message>` | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: when the host's step after registration (`onLoaded`, which fires `ui.resolve`) throws, the mod is unloaded and the line is `<mod>: hooks module did not load: <message>` (§10.8.1 names the line for a failed load step, not a failure after `register`). |
| `src/mods/load/host.ts` | Disposing a host that was given its own signal still aborts its events' `next.signal` (`AbortSignal.any`) | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: disposing a host given its own signal still aborts its events' `next.signal` (`AbortSignal.any`) (§10.8.3 says unloading aborts a mod's dispatches). |
| `src/mods/chain/api-guard.ts` | Within one hook `$.ns.method === $.ns.method`; a top-level typed-array argument is not walked for nesting | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: within one hook `$.ns.method === $.ns.method`, and a top-level typed-array argument is not walked for nesting. |
| `src/mods/events/spec.ts` | `sameData` is iterative and handles cycles (28,000-deep frontmatter) | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: `sameData` is iterative and handles cycles, so 28,000-deep input does not overflow the stack. |
| `src/mods/api/scope.ts`, `tests/mods/api/engine-create-host.test.ts` | Known limit: a scope is one stack per mod. While a lent `engine.create` method awaits, a call the lending mod makes on its own (a timer, background work) runs in the borrowing context; and port calls use the mod's lifetime signal, not the event's abandon signal, so `$.exec`, `$.model` and `$.mcp` work of an abandoned hook runs until the mod unloads | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: known limit: a scope is one stack per mod, so a lending mod's own calls during a lent `engine.create` method run in the borrowing context, and port calls use the mod's lifetime signal; carrying context per call needs async context and `src/` may not import `node:*` (L3). |
| `src/mods/chain/registry.ts`, `src/mods/chain/only.ts`, `src/mods/chain/dispatch.ts`, `src/mods/chain/trace.ts`, `tests/mods/chain/host-scope.test.ts` | A host that keeps one chain for many mods (a mod per project, loaded at different times) gives `ModPluginInfo.position`: mods of one tier run by it (lower first), then by when they registered, and a mod with none runs after those with one; a `caller` stands where its `position` puts it. `DispatchOptions.only` and `ModChain.hears(event, only)` take one name or a set of names (an unregistered name matches nothing; `hears` does not test matchers). `DispatchOptions.trace` is called once when the dispatch ends, also when it failed, with the mods that have a hook for the event (`chain`, once for each hook, in order) and a step for each function hook that answered (`passed` when it answered the value its `next` had already given, `returned` otherwise, with the event it received, its answer and its time) and for Claude Code's own behaviour (`engine`, tier `core`, the index after the last hook); a generator hook, a hook that failed and a core that streams or fails are no step. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: a host that keeps one chain for many mods gives `ModPluginInfo.position`; `DispatchOptions.only`, `ModChain.hears` and `DispatchOptions.trace` are host diagnostics (§10.14 orders one session's mods and describes no shared chain). |
| `src/mods/api/settings-layers.ts` | The default settings adapter uses canonical repair and precedence, permits only supported plugin defaults, and retains original invalid-value floors. A host can provide its own merge port for application-specific fields. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: the default settings adapter uses canonical repair and precedence, permits only supported plugin defaults and keeps original invalid-value floors; a host may give its own merge port. |
| `src/host/session-skills.ts` (`preloadSkills`), `src/skills/lookup.ts` | One lookup serves `expandSkill`, `expandMessage` and `preloadSkills`: a listed command name is claimed first, then the aliases (directory name, frontmatter name) in catalog order. Preload honours `skillOverrides` through `invocationPolicy(...).preloadable` (an `off` or `user-invocable-only` skill is skipped as `disabled` or `not-model-invocable`). A preloaded body is the parsed body, unexpanded: no `!` command runs. A bare name follows oracle 2.1.322 (`src/host/session-skill-preload.ts`, `tests/host/preload-skill-names.test.ts`; `claude -p --plugin-dir` with a private HOME and a local fake Messages API, the subagent's request read for each body): the exact name among skills Claude can invoke, else the agent's own plugin's `<plugin>:<name>` (`PreloadOptions.source`), else the first plugin in load order with one. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: one lookup for expandSkill, expandMessage and preloadSkills, alias tie order, preload body unexpanded (J4.1 and §4.7.1 item 3 decide only which names preload and that the whole content is injected). |
| `src/format/skill-fields.ts` (`readInvocable`), `src/format/skill-types.ts` (`InvocableFrontmatter.frontmatter`), `tests/format/skill-frontmatter-record.test.ts` | Every skill and command keeps its frontmatter record as the YAML parsed it, read-only: unknown keys and keys of another case are kept with no issue, values stay as written (no default, no reading), a command keeps its `name` and `paths` in the record although it does not take them, YAML that does not parse and an inline manifest entry give `{}`. The kit gives no meaning to a key it does not know. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (§4.4.1 item 3 says only that an unknown key is ignored without a report): the kit keeps the parsed record read-only for a host with keys of its own, so it needs no second parse. |
| `src/host/session-host-skills.ts`, `src/format/bundled-skill.ts`, `src/format/skill-name.ts` (`RULES.bundled`), `src/skills/variables.ts` (`ROOTS.bundled`), `src/skills/hook-limits.ts`, `src/host/session-skills.ts`, `src/host/session-content.ts`, `tests/host/host-skills.test.ts` | `SessionOptions.hostSkills` (`() => readonly HostSkill[]`, read at each use and cached per array) are the host's bundled skills, origin `{ kind: 'bundled' }`: their entries come after the plugins' and the folders', so a folder skill of the same command replaces one and a host command beats a plugin skill's bare name; their commands count as taken for a synced skill's short name even while `disableBundledSkills` removes them; the name is the command, refused as a command name is (an empty name, a refused character, `anthropic-skills`), the first of two skills of one name stays and the second is an issue at `hostSkills.<name>`; the frontmatter is read as a `SKILL.md`'s without `name`; with `file` it is a skill whose folder is the file's directory, without one a command; a reader body is called at each expansion; no alias; a frontmatter hook's source is `bundled`; a relative `dir` would sit below the project folder. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: SessionOptions.hostSkills, how a host hands in its bundled skills and where they sit in the entries (§4.5.4 gives bundled-skill rules, not a host API). |
| `src/host/session-skill-entries.ts` (`placeOf`, `entriesOf`), `src/host/session-skill-catalog.ts`, `src/host/session-skill-expand.ts`, `src/skills/variables.ts` (`skillPathIn`), `tests/host/skill-locations.test.ts` | `SessionSkill.file` and `directory` are absolute for every source: a plugin's below `Locations.pluginRoot(id)` of the session, asked once per plugin when the entries of a host generation are built, a folder's as the scan read it, a host skill's as given. A command has a file and no directory, an inline manifest command neither. The expansion uses the row's `directory` for `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}` and the "Base directory" line, and an absolute skill directory is kept as is. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: SessionSkill.file and directory are absolute, placed when a host generation is built (§4.8.2 defines ${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}, not where a host learns a row's place). |
| `src/host/session-first-read.ts`, `src/host/session-folders.ts`, `src/host/session-agents.ts`, `tests/host/first-read.test.ts` | Until the first read of the folders (`reloadSkills`, `addDirectory`, `moveTo`; `reloadAgents`), `skillCatalog().issues`, `skills().issues` and `agentCatalog().issues` carry the warning `the <skills\|agents> folders have not been read yet: call <reload>() before listing them`; a session without `HostPorts.folders` carries none. One rule for both. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: the warning every catalog carries until the host first reads the folders; the kit's catalogs call no port. |
| `src/loading/walk.ts`, `src/loading/discover.ts`, `tests/loading/command-skill-folders.test.ts` | In a commands folder (the default `commands/` or a `commands` path), a folder holding `SKILL.md` is one skill named by that folder and is not walked further; a `commands` path that is such a folder, or that names a `SKILL.md`, is that skill. These skills come before the plugin's `skills/` ones, a folder both scans reach (`commands: "./skills/"`) loads once, and a `.md` command file wins a clash with either | Spec J4.3, J4.27. These skills come before the plugin's `skills/` ones, and a folder both scans reach (`commands: "./skills/"`) loads once. |
| `src/loading/resolve/lsp-startup.ts` | Configured process variables are combined with the resolved host environment; host identity variables win on overlap. An unavailable executable has no process plan. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (J3.7 fixes only that a set variable replaces an inherited one): the process-plan shape, and no plan for an unavailable executable. |
| `src/host/session-workflows.ts`, `src/host/session-skills.ts`, `src/skills/listing.ts`, `tests/host/plugin-workflows.test.ts` | A plugin workflow is a `/` menu row and a model listing entry with its `meta.description`, listed ahead of the skills and commands; it keeps a name a skill or command of its plugin also has (`expandSkill` refuses it: the host runs the script); of two scripts with one `meta.name` the first in the folder listing is kept; a script whose `meta` is not the first statement or has no description is neither; with workflows off none is listed; `skillOverrides` does not apply | Spec J11.3, J11.1. Kit: a workflow is listed ahead of the skills and commands, keeps a name a skill or command of its plugin holds (expandSkill refuses it), skillOverrides does not apply; the folder listing is sorted by code unit. |
| `crates/agent-plugins/src/format/validate/plugin_paths.rs`, `src/format/validate/path-claims.ts` | The `types` path follows the `./` prefix and containment rules of the component paths (the `.d.ts` extension is the field table's) | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (§2.2 types `types` as a path and §10.3.2 reads it, but §2.5 names component paths only): a `..` or absolute path would name a file outside the plugin. |
| `src/format/manifest-schema.ts`, `crates/agent-plugins/src/format/user_config_options.rs` | oracle 2.1.325 (spec §3.4.2, J3.10): `options` with neither a `default` from the list nor `required: true` fails to load; the list holds at least one value, each 1 to 64 code points, no edge space, no control (`Cc`) or format (`Cf`: invisible, bidi) character, no repeat in any letter case; each rule is reported at `options`, `options.<i>` or `default` in the oracle's words and order, a field that may not have `options` alone; every rule also stops loading | Spec J3.10, J3.11. The oracle cases the tests hold are the kit's. |
| `src/loading/channels.ts` | A `channels` entry bound to a key that is not among the plugin's `mcpServers` is an error issue (the plugin still loads, without that channel); the wording | Spec J9.12. The wording is the kit's. |
| `src/loading/resolve/channel.ts` | A channel's options join the plugin's for its server's `env`, and win over a top-level option of the same key | Spec J9.13, J3.21. |
| `src/loading/resolve/user-config-values.ts` | The value checked is the saved one else the `default`; an undeclared key is not checked; a text-typed option takes a string (an array when `multiple`), and the wording of every problem | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (J3.12 gives the value as saved else `default`): a text-typed option takes a string (an array when `multiple`), and the wording of every problem. |
| `src/format/names.ts` | The loader's message for a name that cannot load, `Plugin name "<name>" may not contain spaces, "@", ":", path separators or control characters` (the validator's own errors and the kebab-case warning are the spec's words, `src/format/validate/plugin-name.ts`) | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: §2.3 item 1 says such a name does not load and words only the validator's reports. |
| `src/format/manifest.ts` | The loader's own issue texts for a manifest it reads (`Unknown field "<key>"`, `Invalid "<key>": ...`, `The manifest has no "name"`); `validateManifest` (`src/format/validate/`) reports the schema errors, unknown-field and missing-field warnings in the spec's words | Spec J2.6, J2.7 and §2.11 word the validator's reports; what the loader calls a problem it also reports is the kit's (implementation choice). |
| `src/skills/command-entries.ts`, `crates/agent-plugins/src/format/messages.rs` | The wording of a command entry's `<field> path must start with "./": <source>` and of `agents entries must be .md files: <path>`; a command entry's `source` that leaves the plugin as written (`..`, absolute, a backslash) is judged as on macOS and Linux (`escapedPathIssue` with `platform: 'posix'`), since the port takes `/` paths, and the entry loads nothing | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: the wording of the command-entry path and agents-entry messages, and judging a source's escape as on POSIX because the port takes / paths. |
| `src/mods/load/scan/issue-codes.ts`, `tests/mods/load/scan-issue-codes.test.ts` | **scanIssueCodes** Each scan issue carries a stable `code` from one list; a parse error is `syntax`; the codes group messages by the rule broken, not by wording (the three `Client` module refusals share `client-module`) | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: each scan issue carries a stable `code` from one list (`syntax` for a parse error, `client-module` for the three `Client` refusals), grouping messages by rule (§10.4.2 gives the messages, not codes). |
| `src/format/memory-rule.ts`, `src/skills/path-gate.ts` (`ruleActivatesFor`, `ruleBase`), `tests/format/memory-rule.test.ts`, `tests/skills/rule-gate.test.ts` | **memoryRules** A rule file's `paths` is read by the skill reader's `paths` field and matched by the skill gate's matcher; a project rule's base is the folder before the last `.claude/rules/` of its path (else `cwd`), a user rule's is `cwd`; a file outside the base never matches; a `paths` value of another type is dropped with a warning, as a skill's is; listing the files is the host's | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: memory.md is out of scope as engine, so no requirement says how a rule's base or a bad `paths` is handled; listing the files is the host's. |
| `src/format/frontmatter-write.ts`, `src/format/frontmatter-render.ts`, `tests/format/frontmatter-write.test.ts`, `tests/format/frontmatter-render.test.ts` | **frontmatterWriter** A written file is `---\n<yaml>---\n<body>` with the body as given (no blank line added), keys in the order given (a rendered file: the reference table's order, then the host's own keys), YAML with no line folding and no aliases; an edit keeps a key's place and appends a new one, removes a repeated key's later copies, and refuses a block that does not read; a rendered skill or command leaves out a value equal to its documented default except `shell` | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (§4.4.1 defines reading, not writing): the writer must read back through the kit's reader, and an explicit `shell: bash` differs from the default. |
| `src/format/frontmatter.ts` | A block with no closing `---` is not frontmatter (the whole file is body); the closing marker is the first later line that is only `---`; a leading byte order mark is ignored; an empty block is present with no fields | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (§4.4.1 item 1 fixes the opening line and the next `---`, not an unclosed block, a byte order mark or an empty block). |
| `src/format/frontmatter.ts` | YAML is read with the 1.2 core schema (`yes`/`on` stay strings; a duplicate key does not parse) | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (§4.4.1 item 4 lists the flag spellings, not the YAML schema or duplicate keys). |
| `src/format/frontmatter-values.ts` | How a bad value is reported (the wording of each reason), that a number or boolean is read as text, that an enum matches exactly with no case folding, and that blank text is absent | Spec J4.6. The wording of each reason, a number or boolean read as text, and blank text as absent are the kit's. |
| `src/format/frontmatter.ts` | A block that parses to a list or a single value is `not-a-table`: the file loads with no fields, like unparseable YAML; `line` and `column` count from the opening `---` as line 1 | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (§4.4.1 item 2 names only YAML that does not parse, not a block that parses to a list or a scalar, nor where an error is). |
| `src/format/frontmatter-values.ts` | `readInteger` takes a YAML number or a string of digits that is a safe integer, and reports a value outside `min`/`max` as dropped; `readList` has no default separator mode, each field picks one | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (J4.7 gives the separators per field): how `readInteger` and `readList` take and report a value is the kit's API. |
| `src/loading/resolve/substitute.ts` | The grammar edges: a reference is `${name}` with no braces inside; a `${VAR:-default}` default ends at the first `}` and nests nothing; no escape; an unknown `${...}` stays as written; a `:-default` after a path variable is ignored; one pass, so a substituted value is never rescanned | Spec J3.4, J8.6. A `:-default` after a path variable is ignored. |

| `src/rules/bash/split.ts`, `crates/agent-plugins/src/rules/bash/shell.rs` | The only unparseable commands are those with nothing after `&&` or `\|\|`, an unclosed quote, substitution or bracket, and nesting past the depth limit; the 10,000-character limit is a read-only-set rule and is not applied to rule matching. A deny or ask rule also tries the whole text of an unparseable command. |

An `LSP(path)` rule is read as unreadable: a deny or ask rule covers every LSP call, an allow rule none. A Read deny or ask rule is matched against an LSP call's `filePath` (a relative one resolved against the working directory, as a Read path is) for every operation, `workspaceSymbol` included, with `!` carve-outs as for Read; an LSP call without a `filePath` is covered by it whole (no `!` carve-out reopens it); a Read allow rule does not reach LSP (spec §7.6.6, J7.210). | `src/loading/resolve/shell-prefix.ts` | Under a non-blank `CLAUDE_CODE_SHELL_PREFIX` a stdio server starts as a shell command in Bash (the host may pass the shell), its line every element POSIX-quoted; a blank prefix is no prefix, as for hooks | Spec J8.26, J8.29. |
| `src/format/mcp-reserved.ts`, `src/settings/mcp/candidates.ts` | The reserved MCP server names match exactly, letter case included, and the skipped server is a warning at the file it came from | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (§8.5 item 5 gives the names and the message): an exact, case-sensitive match, and the skipped server a warning at the file it came from. |
| `src/agents/teammate.ts` | A definition without `tools` gives the teammate its default tools (nothing is listed); `SendMessage` and the Task tools are added once, after the list; `restoreTeammate` asks for a trusted folder only for a `project` definition (which stands for an `--add-dir` one too) | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (§5.12.6 gives the parts per display mode): a definition without `tools` gives the teammate its default tools, `SendMessage` and the Task tools are added once after the list, and `restoreTeammate` asks for trust only for a `project` definition. |
| `src/cli/uninstall-data.ts` (`caseInsensitiveFileSystem`) | Without the host's fact the plugins root is taken as case-insensitive, so a case twin keeps the data folder | Spec J3.19. Without the host's fact (`caseInsensitiveFileSystem`) the plugins root is taken as case-insensitive, which never loses data. |
| `src/loading/core/form.ts`, `tests/loading/core/form.test.ts` | A root `plugin.json` makes a core package or a dual package only when it is a contained regular file holding a JSON object whose `$schema` is a canonical core manifest identifier; one linked outside the root is not read, and the root is a Claude Code plugin | Spec J1.1. A root `plugin.json` linked outside the root is not read (core §4.1). |
| `src/format/core/versions.ts`, `tests/format/core/manifest.test.ts` | A `$schema` of the form `https://agent-plugins.org/schemas/<semver>/<document>.schema.json` is a canonical identifier of any release; only 1.1.0 is implemented, and 1.0.0 is not mapped to it, so a 1.0.0 package is a core package rejected as unsupported | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: any release's canonical `$schema` is recognised so an unsupported one can be rejected; only 1.1.0 is implemented and 1.0.0 is not mapped to it. |
| `src/format/core/command.ts`, `tests/format/core/mcp.test.ts` | A bare `command` is one token when it holds no whitespace, no `/` or `\` and no `${`; a `./` command is refused when it holds whitespace, climbs with `..` or names no file | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (core §7.2.1 and §7.3.1 require a single executable token): which characters make a token, and when a `./` command is refused. |
| `src/format/core/remote.ts`, `tests/format/core/mcp.test.ts` | The loopback literals are `127.0.0.0/8` and `[::1]`; an empty user information (`https://@host`) is user information; a header name is an RFC 9110 token, a value visible ASCII, obs-text, spaces and tabs but not at either end | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (core §7.2.1 names loopback ranges and valid header fields): the literals and the header grammar are the kit's reading. |
| `src/format/core/skill.ts`, `tests/format/core/skill-icon.test.ts` | A core skill's frontmatter field outside the six Agent Skills fields is a warning, not a skip; a description of blanks only is empty; a name holds ASCII `a-z`, `0-9` and `-` only | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (Extended §18.3.2 lets a skill carry more fields): an extra field is a warning, a blank description is empty, and a name is ASCII `a-z`, `0-9` and `-`. |
| `src/loading/core/config.ts`, `tests/loading/core/load-core.test.ts` | A `./` command that names no regular file makes its server or handler invalid at load; a `cwd` that does not exist yet is no load problem; a `${PLUGIN_DATA}` `cwd` is contained textually, its links the host's to check when it creates the directory | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (core §7.3.2 item 4): a `./` command naming no regular file is invalid at load; a `cwd` not yet created is no load problem; a `${PLUGIN_DATA}` `cwd` is contained textually. |
| `src/format/core/icon.ts`, `src/loading/core/assets.ts` | Icon candidates come PNG first, then in the order the host lists its formats; a candidate outside the root is left out silently | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (core §4.3.1 lets a client pick any candidate): PNG first, then the host's order, and a candidate outside the root left out silently. |
| `src/format/core/dual.ts`, `tests/format/core/dual.test.ts` | The dual-package check warns for a `version` that is not SemVer, a missing `description` and `$schema` or `extensions` in `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, and errs for a shared field whose values differ, an `author` without `name` and a `homepage` that is no absolute URL | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open (§18.3.1 gives the value that loads in both per field): which findings are errors and the advice are the kit's. |
| `src/host/core-view.ts`, `src/host/state.ts`, `tests/host/core-components.test.ts` | A core package enters a session as a plugin known by its id (`HostCorePackage`), after the Claude Code plugins in the order given, so `enabledPlugins`, managed settings, name conflicts, duplicate ids and dependencies treat it as one; its view has its name and skills and no Claude Code component, manifest or `userConfig`; `HostedPlugin.core` says it is a core package. | Spec J18.15, J18.3. Kit: the base-directory line and the blank line before ARGUMENTS are presentation; no @ references are attached. |

The host leaves `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_*` out of the base environment it starts the process with. | Spec J18.10, J18.11, J18.12. Kit: ResolvedMcpServer with the working directory in core.cwd; the host leaves CLAUDE_PLUGIN_* out of the base environment. |

The dispatch throws when a core `UserPromptSubmit` handler is selected and the host gives no `CoreCallFacts.turnOrigin`; `SessionOptions.agent.id` marks a subagent, where core `SessionStart` does not fire. |
| `src/hooks/core/profiles.ts`, `src/hooks/core/profile-results.ts`, `tests/hooks/core-profiles.test.ts` | Without `CoreCallFacts.mcpToolName` an MCP tool's declared name is the text after the `__` of `mcp__<server>__<tool>` only when the text after `mcp__` holds `__` exactly once, else no profile; without `mcpResult` an MCP call gets no completed profile and `tool_result` is Claude Code's `tool_response`; `Read` text is `readText` exactly, and a text or notebook read without it gets no profile; a shell exit code is the host's, else `0` on `PostToolUse` without `returnCodeInterpretation` (no profile when it is set) and the `Exit code N` line of a failure; a `WebFetch` failure gets no profile. | Spec J18.21, J18.16. The facts come from `CoreCallFacts` (`mcpToolName`, `mcpResult`, `readText`). |
| `src/hooks/core/output.ts`, `src/hooks/core/run.ts`, `src/hooks/core/combine.ts`, `tests/host/core-hooks.test.ts`, `tests/host/core-hook-results.test.ts`, `tests/hooks/core-profiles.test.ts`, `tests/hooks/core-combine.test.ts` | A core handler runs in exec form with stderr apart; any start error, timeout, nonzero status (2 included), non-JSON-whitespace output that is not exactly one object of the event's closed shapes, or `{}` is a failure, reported as a `<Event>[:<tool>] hook error: [<command args>]: <cause>` notice and never a decision, its label the handler's `command` and `args` as written in `hooks.json` (no resolved path, no expansion). The cause: `Failed with non-blocking status code: <first stderr line>` (`no stderr output` when blank; also for a process a signal ended, which has no status), the launch error as it is, `timed out after <N>s; the hook's output was discarded` on `UserPromptSubmit` and `timed out` elsewhere, `wrote output that is not one JSON object`, `wrote an object that is not a <event> result`. Several core denial or continuation reasons are joined by one newline in handler order, empty and absent ones left out; a Stop continuation both sides ask for carries the Claude Code reason first, then the core reasons, in one block. | Spec J18.22, J18.4, J18.5. A process a signal ended is worded as a nonzero status. |
| `src/host/session-core-dispatch.ts`, `src/host/session-engine.ts`, `tests/host/core-hook-results.test.ts` | With no core handler selected a dispatch is the engine's alone; on `Stop` with core handlers the engine runs without the cap and the session applies `core.stopCap` once to the combined outcome; on `PreToolUse` the core sees `updatedInput` only when the combined Claude Code decision is none, `allow` or `ask` and no exit-2 block holds; a dispatch limited by `sourcesOnly` without `plugin` runs no core handler. | Spec J18.1, J18.9. Kit: core.stopCap applied once by the session; sourcesOnly without plugin runs no core handler. |
| `crates/agent-plugins/src/convert/placeholders.rs`, `tests/convert/round-trip.test.ts` | Converting a stdio server drops the source form's own bridge entries (`PLUGIN_ROOT: ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` in `.mcp.json`, `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT: ${PLUGIN_ROOT}` in `mcp.json`, and the `DATA` pair) before adding the target's, keeping a value the author set for that name. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: J18.18 adds the bridge entries; dropping the source form's own pairs before adding the target's, keeping an author's value, keeps a round trip valid (core §9.2). |
| `crates/agent-plugins/src/convert/adapters/registration.rs`, `crates/agent-plugins/src/convert/to_claude_code/hooks.rs`, `crates/agent-plugins/src/convert/to_core/hooks.rs`, `tests/convert/round-trip.test.ts` | A hook registration of exactly the shape this converter writes for its adapter (command `node`, the adapter path first, nothing else; on its own event with the matcher it is registered with) is unwrapped to the handler it runs when converting back; the `PostToolUseFailure` twin of a `PostToolUse` adapter is dropped quietly. Converting a profile back gives the matcher of §18.3.4 (`Bash\|PowerShell`), not the author's order. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: §18.4.1 item 5 and §18.4.4 do not say how to recognise the converter's own adapter registration; the `PostToolUseFailure` twin is dropped quietly and a profile converts back to the matcher of §18.3.4. |
| `crates/agent-plugins/src/convert/adapters/core_profiles_source.rs`, `crates/agent-plugins/src/convert/adapters/core_runner_source.rs`, `crates/agent-plugins/src/convert/to_claude_code/hooks.rs`, `tests/convert/core-adapter.test.ts`, `tests/convert/to-claude-code.test.ts` | The adapter that runs a core handler under Claude Code derives the profiles as `hooks/core` does without the client's facts, omitting what the Claude Code fields do not determine: a shell command's exit code is `0` on `PostToolUse` only without `returnCodeInterpretation` (no profile when it is set) and the `Exit code N` line on a failure; an MCP tool's declared name only when the text after `mcp__` holds `__` once (else no profile); an MCP call's completed profile is omitted (a hook never sees the `CallToolResult`); a text or notebook read gets no profile (the text Claude received is never rebuilt from `file.content`). It fires core `UserPromptSubmit` on every `source`, `system` and an absent one included, and skips only `loop_wakeup` and `schedule_wakeup`; the converter lists every adapted `UserPromptSubmit` handler as `not-converted`, since it also runs on task notifications and automatic continuations. It skips `SessionStart` outside `startup`, `clear`, `fork` or with an `agent_id`, and an interrupted failure; it creates `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA` before the run. | Spec J18.21, J18.23, J18.16 (§18.4.4 items 2 and 5). It also skips `SessionStart` outside `startup`, `clear`, `fork` or with an `agent_id` and an interrupted failure (J18.6, J18.8), and creates `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA` before the run. |
| `src/loading/core/entry-fields.ts`, `src/loading/core/load-package.ts`, `tests/loading/core/form.test.ts` | `loadPluginPackage` given the marketplace entry of a root that loads as a core package reports each component key the entry reader keeps (the chapter's `commands`, `agents`, `skills`, `hooks`, `mcpServers`, `lspServers`, `outputStyles`, themes, and also `userConfig`, `channels`, `settings`, `workflows`) and `strict: false` as an `error` issue whose path is the field, and still loads the package from its own files. | Spec J18.15. The issue path is the field, as `mergeEntryWithManifest` paths its own. |
| `crates/agent-plugins/src/convert/adapters/claude_code_runner_source.rs`, `tests/convert/claude-code-adapter.test.ts` | The adapter that runs a Claude Code hook under the core: a denial or Stop block without a reason gets `Blocked by a hook`; a JSON `decision: "block"` on `PostToolUse` is not expressed (only exit 2 is context); shell form runs `$SHELL` (else `bash`) `-c`, or `pwsh -NoProfile -Command` with the three placeholders as `$env:` references; exec form substitutes `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`, `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}`, `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}` per argument and in the command; `timeout` is enforced by the adapter; any other failing status is nothing on stdout and its stderr. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: §18.4.4 items 1 and 3 give the mapping; the `Blocked by a hook` reason, the shell command lines, the adapter's `timeout` and an unexpressed `PostToolUse` JSON block are the adapter's. |
| `crates/agent-plugins/src/convert/to_core/hook_matchers.rs`, `crates/agent-plugins/src/convert/to_core/hooks.rs`, `tests/convert/to-core-hooks.test.ts` | Converting to the core: a `SessionStart` hook that matches everything converts and is reported as running on new sessions only; `startup\|clear\|fork` in any order converts silently; any other SessionStart matcher is not converted; matchers on `UserPromptSubmit` and `Stop` are ignored as Claude Code ignores them; every converted `PostToolUse` hook is reported as also running on failed calls; `timeout` and `shell` are kept by the adapter, `statusMessage` is dropped and reported. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: §18.4.3 item 4 names the tool matchers only; how `SessionStart` matchers, the ignored matchers and `timeout`, `shell`, `statusMessage` are reported follows J18.6 and J18.8. |
| `crates/agent-plugins/src/convert/to_core/skills.rs`, `crates/agent-plugins/src/convert/to_core/mcp.rs`, `tests/convert/to-core.test.ts` | Skills outside `skills/<name>/` are reported, not copied, and Claude Code fields are reported, not stripped; a server is not converted, rather than written without the field, when it has a field the core lacks (`headersHelper`, `oauth`, `timeout`, `alwaysLoad`); a stdio `command` converts only as `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/<path>` or a bare name without whitespace, `/`, `\\` or `$`. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: §18.4.3 items 2 and 3 make copying and stripping optional; a server with a field the core lacks is not converted, since dropping it changes how it connects. |
| `src/convert/namespaces.ts`, `src/convert/convert-package.ts`, `src/convert/judge.ts`, `tests/convert/convert-package.test.ts` | `convertPluginPackage` loads a core package with every namespace found in `extensions` of the three documents and every top-level directory named as two or more dot-separated labels, so each is reported; a dual package is not converted; a written path that exists stops; a Claude Code output is stopped by any `claude plugin validate` error of the folder as it would be, a core output by any core loader error outside `skills/`; Claude Code components a core package carried as ordinary files are reported as now loading; the source loader's errors are `not-converted` notes. | Implementation choice; the spec leaves it open: §18.4.1 items 1, 3 and 4 give the rules; how `convertPluginPackage` finds namespaces, stops on an existing path and on each validator's errors, and reports, is the converter's. |

## Disagreements with the spec

Rows of the table above whose decision differs from the specification, until each is settled in
the spec (E1a) or in the code. None at the pinned revision.

## Rows the spec now states

The specification states these as normative requirements, so they are no longer implementation
judgments. They stay here so nothing is lost, and can go once the tests that cover them cite the
section.

| Where                         | Judgment                                                                                          | Why                                                                                       |
| ----------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `src/mods/load/host-support.ts`, `src/mods/events/command.ts` | `command.run` looks up the command as fired: a hook may not rewrite `e.command` (`command.run: a hook may not rewrite e.command, only e.args`) | Spec §10.18.1 (normative). |
| `src/format/manifest-schema.ts` | `author`, `dependencies` objects and `commands` entries are not strict (unknown keys dropped) | Spec §2.1 (normative). |
| `src/loading/resolve/surfaces.ts` | `${VAR}` expands only in MCP `command`, `args`, `env`, `url`, `headers` and in monitor `command`; elsewhere (hooks, LSP, `headersHelper`, content) it is left to the process | Spec §8.4.2, §9.2.2 (normative). |
| `src/loading/resolve/data-id.ts` | A letter is `A-Z` or `a-z`: a non-ASCII letter (for example `\u00e9`) in a plugin identifier becomes `-` | Spec §3.1.2 (normative). |
| `src/rules/path/cd.ts` | `Cd(<pattern>)` compiles to a whole-path expression: `*` is within one segment, `**` between segments is zero or more segments, a trailing `/**` also matches its root; a relative pattern is under the cwd | Spec §13.8 (normative). |
| `src/rules/target/mcp.ts` | `mcpToolName` normalises the server name and leaves the tool name as the server declares it; a bare `Bash` or `WebFetch` deny rule (no specifier) covers the Cowork tools `mcp__workspace__bash` and `mcp__workspace__web_fetch`, an ask rule does not | Spec §13.6, §13.7 (normative). |
| `crates/agent-plugins/src/format/hooks_handlers.rs` | `continueOnBlock` is read on prompt hooks | Spec §7.3.6 (normative). |
| `src/hooks/handlers/remote/mcp-tool.ts` | A server that is not connected after the wait, a call the client could not make and a tool that fails are one `mcp` run with `isError: true` and a text; the server name is passed as the hook writes it, never rewritten | Spec §7.3.5 (normative). |
| `crates/agent-plugins/src/hooks/output/messages.rs` | `DirectoryAdded`'s `systemMessage` goes to Claude's context on `slash_command` and nowhere on `register_repo_root`; the other events' go to the user | Spec §7.5.27 (normative). |
| `src/mods/chain/validate.ts` | Only two hooks on one event with no matcher (none, or one with no filtering field) are "registered twice without a matcher"; the module is refused whole, none of its hooks is added; the issue path is `hooks:<event>` | Spec §10.13.3, §10.4.2 (normative). |
| `src/mods/chain/wildcard.ts` | A hook registered under `*` or `classic.*` handles only the events whose form fits it: an async function never sees a streaming event and an async generator never sees another | Spec §10.13.3 (normative). |
| `src/mods/chain/tiers.ts` | `next.to` takes a tier strictly after the caller's own, and a mod that may not call it (any outside `prepend` and `append`) is refused with the kit's wording; a refusal is a rejection of the returned promise or stream | Spec §10.6.1 (normative). |
| `src/mods/chain/api-guard.ts` | A call refused for nesting rejects with `$.<namespace>.<method> argument: nesting deeper than 28000 levels is not supported`, even for a method that is synchronous | Spec §10.11 (normative). |
| `src/protocol/mods-events.ts` | `plugin.name` and `plugin.root` are in `MODS_API` (the page lists them in the `$.plugin` row) but are not in `API_EVENT_NAMES`: `*` and an `fs.read`-style hook never see them | Spec §10.19.7 (normative). |
| `src/mods/events/skill-prompt-event.ts` | `e.pluginName` is set only when `source` is `plugin`; `e.frontmatter` is `{}` for a skill with no file or no frontmatter; `e.frontmatterProblem` is the frontmatter parser's problem text (`is a list or a single value, not a table`, or the YAML error), so a table that parses is never partial | Spec §10.17.2 (normative). |
| `src/mods/events/autocomplete.ts` | `autocompleteRows` puts Claude Code's own rows first and the mods' rows after them, and an answer of the wrong shape adds none; that Claude Code "doesn't wait for the hook" is the host's to implement when it fires the event | Spec §10.17 (normative). |
| `src/mods/api/rules.ts` | A hook on a mods API call that no event of Claude Code's shares the name of (`fs.read`, `store.get`...) fails like a throw when it answers neither `{ value }`, `{ deny }` nor `next(e)` (a hook on the stream `process.spawn` answers what `next(e)` does, the command's own result, and only its `{ deny }` is special); the wording `returned neither { value } nor { deny }` is test.md's, the rest is the kit's | Spec §10.15, §10.19.7 (normative). |
| `src/mods/ui/elements/box.ts` | A `Box` takes the flexbox layout props `flexWrap`, `flexGrow`, `flexShrink`, `alignItems`, `alignSelf` and `justifyContent` beside `flexDirection`; a spacing value that is not a number of cells (0 or more) or a step name fails the tree | Spec §10.10, §10.24 (normative). |
| `src/mods/ui/elements/table.ts` | An element an app cannot draw is absent from the table `$.ui.resolve(e)` gives, and a tree that holds one is refused; the VS Code extension's ignored props are dropped from what it draws (`drawnProps`) | Spec §10.10 (normative). |
| `src/mods/chain/failure.ts`, `src/mods/types.ts` | A hook whose result has the wrong shape is skipped with `returned the wrong shape (<rule>)` (the documented line); only a result nested too deep is `refused`; `EventRule.shapeProblem` receives the event as fired so a tree is judged for its site and app | Spec §10.24, §10.41.1 (normative). |
| `src/mods/types.ts` | `$.ui.resolve(e)` answers at once with the element table, not a promise | Spec §10.23 (normative). |
| `src/mods/ui/controls/route.ts` | A typed value or a pick is in `e.value` of `ui.input` and `ui.select`, and `ui.press` carries `surface`; typing with Enter reaches `onSubmit`, typing alone `onInput`; a callback that throws is an issue, not a throw | Spec §10.19.4 (normative). |
| `src/mods/load/policy.ts` | `--safe-mode`, `--bare` and `disableAllHooks` refusals read `installed plugins load no hooks module in this mode (--safe-mode)`, `installed plugins that are not managed load no hooks module in this mode (--bare)`, `disableAllHooks in managed settings`, `only managed plugins and built-in plugins run` | Spec §10.42.1 (normative). |
| `src/mods/load/policy-input.ts` | The guard's options are read only from managed `pluginConfigs['cc-plugin-sec-default@builtin'].options`; for any other plugin a managed entry wins over the user's | Spec §14.9.6 (normative). |
| `src/mods/load/guard.ts` | With managed settings the guard cannot read, every user's mod is refused (`MANAGED_UNREADABLE`); a user's mod that approved a call a deny rule refuses is named in the message as the first approver | Spec §14.9.6 (normative). |
| `src/mods/load/scan/matcher-label.ts` | A matcher prints as its fields joined by commas, as `{ command: tally }` becomes `command=tally` | Spec §10.4.3 (normative). |
| `src/mods/load/scan/top-level.ts` | A `const` arrow function at the top level counts as a top-level function that may receive `$` (`(via fn)`) | Spec §10.4.2 (normative). |
| `src/mods/load/policy.ts` (`hooksModulesOff`), `src/testing/mods/runner.ts` | The lines are Claude Code 2.1.325's (Agent Plugins Extended §10.42.1): `hooks modules are turned off here (disableAllHooks, allowManagedHooksOnly or a policy)` for `disableAllHooks` in either file or managed `allowManagedHooksOnly`, and `<dir>: no hooks module to load; there is no hooks/hooks.json naming one in "modules"` (`<dir>` the given directory, `.` when none), which exits 0; a directory with a hooks module and no test file exits 1 with `no test file found` | Spec §10.40.2 (normative). |
| `src/mods/load/host.ts` | The host fires `session.start` for each loaded mod after `load` and `reload`, and for one mod after `reloadMod`, with `{ surface, isInteractive, cwd }`; the test kit turns it off | Spec §10.19.2 (normative). |
| `src/rules/tools/availability.ts` | A deny rule with a glob (`mcp__*`, `End*`) and no specifier removes the tools it matches, like a bare name; a deny rule with a specifier, a parameter rule, an ask rule and an allow rule remove nothing | Spec §13.3 (normative). |
| `src/rules/tools/availability.ts` | An allow rule saves `EndConversation` from `*` only when it names it exactly (bare, no glob, no specifier) | Spec §7.6.3 (normative). |

| `src/mods/chain/next.ts`, `src/mods/chain/link-trace.ts`, `src/mods/chain/select.ts`, `tests/mods/chain/next-trace.test.ts` | declarations 2.1.321. **next.event, next.is and next.trace**: every `next` has `event` (the dispatch's event name, claude-code/index.d.ts:7018), `is(pattern, e)` (whether a name, `*`, `ns.*` or `!pattern` selects the event, :7013 and `Selected`, :11516) and `trace` (:7039): what settled beneath the hook on its latest `next()` call, an entry per link beneath (`TraceEntry`, :14799: `index`, `plugin`, `tier`, `event`, `outcome`, `reason?`, `ms`, `chunks?`, `received`, `returned`), nearest first, the engine's last, empty before `next`, filled when `next` rejected, frozen. Outcomes (`TraceOutcome`, :14851): `passed` when a hook returned what its latest `next` resolved to, `returned` otherwise, `skipped` for a failure before `next` and for each link a `next.to` went beneath (`reason: bypassed by <plugin>`), `kept` for a failure after `next`, `expired` for a timeout without a `.catch` answer, `caught` for a `.catch` answer, `rejected` for a link that rejected. `ms` is the link's wall time less its settled `next` runs'. `next.to` takes `user` too (`TargetTier`, :13622): from a prepend hook it skips the prepend hooks below it. `next` carries no host fields (`nextFields` is gone: the declarations type every field); a runtime in another isolate gets `HookCall.name` and `HookCall.trace()` and answers `next.is` with `selects`. | Spec §10.6.1 (normative). |
| `src/mods/chain/judge.ts`, `src/mods/chain/next.ts`, `src/mods/chain/failure.ts`, `tests/mods/chain/declared-next.test.ts` | declarations 2.1.321. **What a hook returns, `next` with no argument, `next(e)` on a streaming event**: a function hook that returns `undefined` fails as `wrong-shape` (`it returned nothing: return next(e) or a result`), so its `.catch` handler answers, else its latest `next`'s result stands, else it is skipped (`Next`, claude-code/index.d.ts:6970: "returning nothing is a failure"); a hook that passes on `next`'s own `undefined` is not judged. `next()` with no argument rejects with `next() takes the event: the <event> hook of <mod> called it with none` (:6969); `next(undefined)` passes `undefined` on. A streaming hook that returns nothing lets its latest `next(e)`'s result stand (`StreamHookBody`, :13444); `next(e)` there is a `ModHookStream` whose `result` settles at the stream's end and rejects with `next(e): the stream was closed before its end` (`HookStream`, :5487). | Spec §10.13, §10.6.1 (normative). |
| `src/format/output-style-command.ts` | Over Remote Control or a relayed message the list keeps the built-in rows and the current mark only on one of them; in a session that does not load `local`, the list is still shown and only a selection is refused | Spec §6.1.4 (normative). |
| `crates/agent-plugins/src/convert/to_claude_code/manifest.rs`, `tests/convert/to-claude-code.test.ts` | A core name `claude plugin validate` refuses (a reserved name) stops a conversion to Claude Code; its warnings (not kebab-case, J18.19) are `reported` notes. | Spec §18.4.1 item 2 (normative). |
